# PQC Today capability coverage matrix

> PQC Today executes selected public reference vectors, standards tests, conformance cases, and implementation probes. A passing result is evidence only for the identified test, operation, parameters, implementation build, and target. It is not an ACVTS verdict, a CAVP/CMVP certificate, or proof of exhaustive conformance.

Generated by `scripts/generate-coverage-matrix.ts` from the runtime mechanism inventory, the reviewed capability map and the registered test inventory. Machine-readable copy: `coverage-matrix.json`. A status says a registered test exists for the cell; it is not a pass.

## Headline

- C++: 1635 advertised capability cells (165 mechanisms). Positive: 98 covered, 708 sampled, 829 untested. Negative: 35 covered, 25 sampled, 1575 untested. 27 further cells unsupported. WebAssembly runs: 922 cells with a recorded pass, 438 with a recorded fail, 0 with a recorded skip (not run, never a pass).
- Rust: 1025 advertised capability cells (172 mechanisms). Positive: 102 covered, 430 sampled, 493 untested. Negative: 35 covered, 25 sampled, 965 untested. 637 further cells unsupported. WebAssembly runs: 641 cells with a recorded pass, 554 with a recorded fail, 0 with a recorded skip (not run, never a pass).

## Rules and definitions

- **cell** — A capability cell is one (mechanism, operation, parameter set, sign variant). Mechanism, operation (from CKF_ flags) and key-size range are DISCOVERED from the running engine (C_GetMechanismList + C_GetMechanismInfo); parameter sets, sign variants and boundaries are DECLARED in capability-map.json and kept only when the parameter set falls inside the engine-reported key-size range.
- **denominator** — Denominator (per engine) = advertised capability cells: the engine lists the mechanism, sets the CKF_ flag for the operation, and the declared parameter set falls inside its reported key-size range. Cells the engine does not advertise, and declared capabilities no PKCS #11 mechanism can express, are counted separately as unsupported and shown — never dropped.
- **status** — Cell status = the strongest evidence class among registered test cases that exercise the cell on that engine (order: acvts-issued > nist-reference > standard-kat > oracle > differential > round-trip > behavior-only). No registered case = untested. Not advertised = unsupported. A status says a test EXISTS; it is not a pass.
- **covered** — Covered (per polarity) = at least 2 distinct registered cases of that polarity exercise the cell AND, for positive, at least one of them checks an externally expected value (acvts-issued, nist-reference, standard-kat or oracle). A cell is covered overall only when positive, negative, boundary and state-error are all covered.
- **sampled** — Sampled = at least one registered case, but the covered rule is not met. One happy-path case is always "sampled", never "covered" (plan Q5).
- **polarity** — Polarities are counted separately: a positive case never counts toward negative, boundary or state-error. Boundary = a registered case whose recorded parameters hit a declared boundary of the cell (e.g. context length 0 or 255, empty message); it may also be a positive or negative case. State-error = a case asserting a PKCS #11 state or argument error.
- **parity** — Parity = both engines have a RECORDED PASS of the same registered case on the same artifact kind. Skipped, unsupported and not-run never count as a pass, and a recorded result counts only for the exact artifact (sha256) the mechanism inventory records. Rows with no such pair are "not established".
- **artifacts** — Artifact kinds are reported separately: wasm (the two shipped WebAssembly engines — registered tests run in the browser), native, hardware. Native and hardware targets have not been run: they are "not run" and never counted as pass.
- **dimensions** — Plan G-3 — two further columns per cell, from the same registered cases. Algorithm correctness = cases whose expected value is checked (acvts-issued, nist-reference, standard-kat, oracle, differential, round-trip; state-error cases excluded); covered when at least 2 distinct cases exist and one has an externally expected value. PKCS #11 API behaviour = cases that assert only PKCS #11 behaviour (behavior-only evidence: return codes, operation state, buffers, attributes) plus every state-error case; covered when at least 2 distinct cases exist and one is a state-error case. Neither column says a test passed.
- **inventory** — Per engine and artifact kind the matrix counts, separately: advertised cells (the denominator — C_GetMechanismList × operation × parameter set × variant), registered cells (≥ 1 registered case, by evidence status), cells with a recorded pass, with a recorded fail, and with a recorded skip (the engine did not advertise a mechanism the case needs, so it was not run), unsupported cells (not advertised) and untested cells (advertised, no registered case). A skip is never a pass and is never folded into untested.
- **numerator** — Numerators (per engine, per polarity) = advertised cells whose level is covered, sampled or untested under the rules below; covered + sampled + untested = the denominator. Per-status numerators count advertised cells by their strongest registered evidence class.

## Artifact kinds and parity

- **wasm** — registered; partly recorded. Registered tests target the shipped WebAssembly engines. Recorded results: 4532 from errorPathProbes (every registered error-path case, dual engine) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub 844e0b0e1); 94 from katRunner (every registered KatKind) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub 844e0b0e1); 1356 from useAcvpSuite (all categories, dual engine) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub 844e0b0e1). Tests without a recorded result are never reported as passed.
- **native** — not-run. macOS, Linux x86-64 and Linux Arm64 native builds have not been run through these tests (plan H-2). Never counted as pass.
- **hardware** — not-run. i.MX 95 and KV260 targets have not been run through these tests (plan H-3). Never counted as pass.
- **parity (positive)** — 461 rows with recorded parity, 38 divergent, 514 not established, 649 advertised by one engine only.

Hub adapter bug, not an engine defect. Fixed by the X.690 long-form length (softhsm/helpers derOctetString); the re-recorded run shows the case passing on both engines. Kept on record until the release freeze re-captures evidence on the browser host. |

| C++: sign/verify inits accept a key of the wrong type (CKR_OK instead of CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT) | open | unassigned | G-8 | C++ (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 7795799b) | — | C_SignInit and C_VerifyInit with an AES secret key return CKR_OK for 33 mechanisms (CKM_ECDSA and the 9 CKM_ECDSA_SHA*, CKM_EDDSA, CKM_RSA_PKCS, CKM_RSA_X_509, CKM_RSA_PKCS_PSS and every CKM_*_RSA_PKCS / CKM_*_RSA_PKCS_PSS hash variant); C_MessageSignInit / C_MessageVerifyInit do the same for the 30 ECDSA/RSA message-sign mechanisms; C_DeriveKey(CKM_HKDF_DERIVE) accepts an EC private key. PKCS #11 v3.2 §5.13.1, §5.15.1, §5.14.1, §5.16.1, §5.18.5 list CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT and §5.1.6 defines it ("The specified key is not the correct type of key to use with the specified mechanism"). ML-DSA/SLH-DSA/HMAC/CMAC inits reject the same key correctly. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened. |
| C++: CKR_KEY_FUNCTION_NOT_PERMITTED returned where CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT has priority | open | unassigned | G-8 | C++ (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 7795799b) | — | C_SignRecoverInit / C_VerifyRecoverInit (CKM_RSA_PKCS, CKM_RSA_X_509) and C_EncapsulateKey (CKM_ML_KEM all three sets, CKM_ECDH1_DERIVE P-256/384/521) given a key of the wrong type (which also lacks the usage attribute) return CKR_KEY_FUNCTION_NOT_PERMITTED. §5.1.6: CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT "has a higher priority than CKR_KEY_FUNCTION_NOT_PERMITTED"; §5.18.8 does not list CKR_KEY_FUNCTION_NOT_PERMITTED for C_EncapsulateKey at all. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened. |

| Rust: malformed parameter / key handle 0 return CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD | open | unassigned | G-8 | Rust (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 417c47a2) | — | A 1-byte parameter returns CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD from C_EncryptInit / C_DecryptInit for 11 mechanisms (CKM_AES_CBC, _CBC_PAD, _CCM, _CFB1, _CFB8, _CFB128, _GCM, _OFB, _XTS, CKM_CHACHA20, CKM_CHACHA20_POLY1305; CKM_AES_CTR answers correctly) instead of CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID (§5.1.6; C_EncryptInit §5.8.1 does not list CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD), and C_DeriveKey with hBaseKey = 0 returns CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD for CKM_ECDH1_DERIVE, CKM_ECDH1_COFACTOR_DERIVE and CKM_HKDF_DERIVE instead of CKR_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID ("0 is never a valid key handle", §5.1.6). Review note as for the C++ entry: §5.1.7 lets a token return any applicable code where the function lists CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened. |
| Rust: C_EncapsulateKey / C_DecapsulateKey create a token object in a read-only session | open | unassigned | G-8 | Rust (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 417c47a2) | — | With CKA_TOKEN = CK_TRUE in the new-key template, C_EncapsulateKey and C_DecapsulateKey on a session opened without CKF_RW_SESSION return CKR_OK (CKM_ML_KEM all three sets, CKM_ECDH1_DERIVE P-256/384/521). §5.18.8 / §5.18.9 list CKR_SESSION_READ_ONLY (§5.1.6). C_GenerateKey(Pair), C_DeriveKey and C_UnwrapKey refuse correctly. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened. |
| Rust: a second C_MessageEncryptInit / C_MessageDecryptInit while one is active returns CKR_OK | open | unassigned | G-8 | Rust (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 417c47a2) | — | CKM_AES_GCM (AES-128, AES-256): the second message-based init on the same session returns CKR_OK instead of CKR_OPERATION_ACTIVE (§5.9.1 / §5.11.1 return values, §5.1.6). Message-sign/verify and every single-part init refuse correctly. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened. |
| Rust: Edwards / Montgomery key-pair generation accepts an inconsistent CKA_KEY_TYPE | open | unassigned | G-8 | Rust (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 417c47a2) | — | C_GenerateKeyPair(CKM_EC_EDWARDS_KEY_PAIR_GEN, Ed25519/Ed448) and (CKM_EC_MONTGOMERY_KEY_PAIR_GEN, X25519/X448) with CKA_KEY_TYPE = CKK_RSA in the public template return CKR_OK. §5.18.2: a template key type inconsistent with the mechanism "fails and returns the error code CKR_TEMPLATE_INCONSISTENT". Every other key-generation mechanism refuses correctly. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened. |
| Rust: advertised cells whose operation does not execute | open | unassigned | G-8 | Rust (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 417c47a2) | — | The G-2/G-8 probes found advertised capability cells that fail on a valid key: CKM_ECDSA_SHA1 and CKM_ECDSA_SHA224 — C_SignInit CKR_OK then C_Sign CKR_MECHANISM_INVALID on P-256, P-384, P-521 and secp256k1; CKM_ECDSA (raw) on P-521 with a 32-byte input — C_Sign CKR_FUNCTION_FAILED (§6.3 allows any input length for raw ECDSA); AES-192 (inside the advertised 16–32 byte range) — CKM_AES_CBC_PAD C_Encrypt returns CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT and CKM_AES_GCM C_MessageEncryptInit CKR_KEY_SIZE_RANGE; CKM_EC_KEY_PAIR_GEN_W_EXTRA_BITS on secp256k1 (in the advertised 256–521 range) — CKR_CURVE_NOT_SUPPORTED. Either the operation or the advertisement (C_GetMechanismInfo range / flags, plan G-7) is wrong. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened. |
| PKCS #11 v3.2 gives no single key-handle / key-type code for C_DecapsulateKey — not probed | open | unassigned | G-8 | C++ + Rust — specification | — | §5.18.9 lists CKR_UNWRAPPING_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID and CKR_UNWRAPPING_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT (not CKR_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID / CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT), while §5.1.6 says the UNWRAPPING_* codes "can only be returned by C_UnwrapKey". With no single specified value there is no key-handle or key-type probe for C_DecapsulateKey (the engines differ: C++ returns CKR_UNWRAPPING_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID for handle 0, Rust CKR_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID). Likewise C_WrapKey, C_UnwrapKey, C_DeriveKey and C_EncapsulateKey require the usage attribute but do not list CKR_KEY_FUNCTION_NOT_PERMITTED, so no key-usage probe exists for them. Specification ambiguity, not an engine defect. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened. |
| Rust engine refuses AES-GCM IVs other than 96 bits | open | unassigned | E (AES-GCM) / G-9 | Rust (wasm) | — | The NIST ACVP-Server ACVP-AES-GCM-1.0 sample (aesgcm_acvp_test, workbench §1b) has two groups with a 120-bit IV (tg2 encrypt, tg4 decrypt). The Rust engine refuses them at C_EncryptInit/C_DecryptInit with CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID (rust/src/ffi.rs ~8158 at 7795799b: 'AES-GCM requires a 12-byte nonce'); SP 800-38D and PKCS#11 v3.2 §6.13.2 allow other IV lengths and the C++ engine passes all 60 cases. The 30 Rust rows fail, including tg4's upstream authentication failures, which are refused before the tag is checked and therefore do not count as rejections. Engine limitation or advertisement to narrow — a decision for the engine owner. |
| Rust engine accepts P-224 keys but cannot verify P-224 ECDSA signatures | open | unassigned | E (ECDSA) / G-7 / G-9 | Rust (wasm) | — | ECDSA-SigVer-FIPS186-5 P-224 groups (ecdsa_sigver_acvp_test, workbench §4b): C_CreateObject accepts the P-224 public key, then every C_Verify returns CKR_SIGNATURE_LEN_RANGE for the 56-byte r\|\|s — the Rust crate set has no P-224 (no p224 dependency at 7795799b) although CKM_ECDSA* advertise ulMinKeySize 112. All 28 Rust P-224 rows fail (valid cases rejected; invalid cases refused for the wrong reason, which the section does not count as a rejection). C++ passes all 112 ECDSA cases. P-224 is also outside the capability map's EC curve set, so these cases map to no capability cell; their results are recorded. |
| HMAC keys below the advertised ulMinKeySize are accepted by both engines | open | unassigned | G-1 / G-7 | C++ + Rust (wasm) — advertisement | — | C_GetMechanismInfo advertises ulMinKeySize = the digest length in bytes for every CKM_<hash>_HMAC[_GENERAL] (e.g. 32 for SHA2-256), yet both engines compute and verify NIST HMAC 2.0 cases with shorter keys (down to 1 byte) correctly (hmac_acvp_matrix_test, workbench §2b; each row records keyInAdvertisedRange). FIPS 198-1 allows any key length, so the results are correct; the advertisement understates what the engines accept. Recorded, not enforced. |
| Classical NIST reference samples cover only what the pinned upstream samples register | open | unassigned | E | C++ + Rust | — | The pinned ACVP-Server@975de31e samples bound what WS-E can claim: ACVP-AES-GCM-1.0 registers AES-128 only (IV 96/120, tag 128/32, payload/AAD 0/120 bits) — AES-192/256-GCM, 64-bit tags and other lengths have no NIST case; HMAC 2.0 registers macLen 80..160 bits — no full-length (untruncated) tag, so CKM_<hash>_HMAC (non-general) has no NIST case; EDDSA-SigVer-1.0 carries no context string (Ed25519ctx / Ed448 contexts not claimed); ECDSA SHA2-512/256 and SHAKE groups have no PKCS#11 v3.2 CKM_ECDSA_<hash> mechanism (skip rows). |
| Rust engine answers a wrong-length AES-CBC IV with CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD | open | unassigned | G-8 / G-9 | Rust (wasm) | — | Product-authored probe aescbc-probe-iv15 (workbench §12b): C_EncryptInit(CKM_AES_CBC) with a 15-byte IV. The C++ engine returns CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID; the Rust engine returns CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD, which PKCS#11 v3.2 §5.8.1 does not list for C_EncryptInit (CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID is the listed code for a bad mechanism parameter). The Rust row stays red; the probe pins the spec code for both engines. |
| Rust engine cannot verify RSA signatures whose public exponent exceeds 2^33 - 1 | open | unassigned | E (RSA) / G-9 | Rust (wasm) | — | RSA-SigVer-FIPS186-5 (rsa_sigver_acvp_test, workbench §4d): the upstream 4096-bit PKCS#1 v1.5 group (e = 0xC9986A9C84FEE9, 56 bits) and the 2048-bit PSS/SHA3-256 group (e = 0x0ACC245201D531, 52 bits) import, but every C_Verify returns CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT on the Rust engine — rsa::RsaPublicKey::new (rust/src/crypto/handlers.rs ~2895 at 7795799b) refuses exponents above 2^33 - 1. FIPS 186-5 §A.1.1 allows 2^16 < e < 2^256. 12 Rust rows fail (valid cases rejected, invalid ones refused for the wrong reason); C++ passes all 24. The groups were chosen by the deterministic subset policy (first group per combination), not by result. |
| Rust engine's CKM_PKCS5_PBKD2 implements only the HMAC-SHA-256/384/512 PRFs | open | unassigned | E (PBKDF2) / G-7 / G-9 | Rust (wasm) | — | PBKDF 1.0 (pbkdf2_acvp_test, workbench §18b): the pinned NIST sample registers HMAC-SHA2-224 only. The Rust engine answers CKP_PKCS5_PBKD2_HMAC_SHA224 with CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD (rust/src/ffi.rs ~11138 at 7795799b: prf match arms SHA256/384/512 only, every other PRF -> CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD, a code that does not name the parameter); CKM_PKCS5_PBKD2 is advertised without any PRF restriction. All 5 Rust rows fail; C++ passes all 5, including the 1-iteration case that the Rust iteration floor (pbkdf2-min-iterations-divergence) would refuse anyway. |
| KMAC C_Verify accepts only the default MAC length, whatever ulOutputLen says | open | unassigned | E (KMAC) / G-9 | C++ + Rust (wasm) — vendor mechanism | — | KMAC-128 1.0 MVT (kmac_acvp_test, workbench §35b): the valid case tc799 has a 478-byte MAC; both engines accept CK_PQCTODAY_KMAC_PARAMS.ulOutputLen = 478 at C_VerifyInit but C_Verify returns CKR_SIGNATURE_LEN_RANGE because the signature length is checked against the mechanism's fixed default (32 bytes for KMAC-128; rust/src/ffi.rs ~7295-7336 at 7795799b lists CKM_KMAC_128/256 among the fixed-length MACs). The invalid case tc771 (a 32-byte MAC) is rejected with CKR_SIGNATURE_INVALID on both. The pinned KMAC-256 sample has no byte-aligned non-XOF case, so KMAC-256 still has no NIST case (the older kmac_test KMAC256 case stays quarantined). Vendor mechanism, both engines. |
| Classical families still without NIST ACVP-Server cases after WS-E | open | unassigned | E | C++ + Rust | — | Not yet sourced from the pinned ACVP-Server samples (remediation plan WS-E, P1 breadth): HKDF (KDA-HKDF-Sp800-56Cr2 needs fixedInfo assembly), SP 800-108 KBKDF (KDF-1.0 counter/feedback), ANSI X9.63 KDF (kdf-components-ansix9.63: PKCS#11 exposes it only inside CKM_ECDH1_DERIVE, which takes no caller-supplied Z, so the upstream (Z, sharedInfo) -> key mapping cannot be driven), ECDSA/EdDSA KeyVer (C_CreateObject is not a key-validation operation) and ECDSA/EdDSA SigGen verify-back, the alternate-version SHA-2 MCT (SHA2-256/512/512-256), full 100-iteration MCTs (only outer iteration 0 is checked for SHA-2/SHA-3 standard MCT and AES-CBC MCT), and SHA LDT. The existing HKDF/PBKDF2/X9.63/KBKDF workbench rows keep their published-standard / oracle / round-trip labels. |
